# 5/3/1 BBB (Boring But Big)

Created by Jim Wendler as an accessory template for his 5/3/1 strength program, BBB (Boring But Big) adds hypertrophy volume on top of the strength-focused main work. The genius of BBB is its simplicity: the main lift follows the 5/3/1 wave (building strength and neural efficiency); the supplemental 5×10 on the same lift at 50% of Training Max (TM) provides the metabolic stress and muscle damage required for hypertrophy. Two different adaptations, one lift, one session.

## Who This Is For

- Intermediate strength athletes (powerlifters, Olympic lifters) who want to add muscle mass without abandoning their strength foundation
- Bodybuilders with basic strength experience who want a structured framework with clear progression rules
- Athletes who dislike excessive exercise variation and prefer training a few movements very well

## The Training Max (TM) Principle

The TM is **not** your true 1RM. It is set at **90% of your true 1RM**. This is the most common BBB mistake — setting TM at true 1RM makes all prescribed percentages too heavy, causes AMRAP sets to fail early, and generates excessive fatigue.

Example (Bench Press 1RM = 100 kg):
- TM = 100 × 0.90 = **90 kg**
- Week 1, Set 3 (85% of TM): 90 × 0.85 = **76.5 kg** — this is where the AMRAP happens

## Weekly Schedule

| Day | Main Lift | BBB Supplemental |
|-----|-----------|-----------------|
| Monday | Overhead Press (5/3/1) | Bench Press 5×10 at 50% TM |
| Tuesday | Deadlift (5/3/1) | Bent-over Row 5×10 at 50% DL TM |
| Thursday | Bench Press (5/3/1) | Overhead Press 5×10 at 50% OHP TM |
| Friday | Back Squat (5/3/1) | Deadlift 5×10 at 50% DL TM |

## The 5/3/1 Wave

| Week | Sets × Reps | %TM |
|------|------------|-----|
| 1 (5+ Week) | 5 / 5 / 5+ | 65 / 75 / 85% |
| 2 (3+ Week) | 3 / 3 / 3+ | 70 / 80 / 90% |
| 3 (1+ Week) | 5 / 3 / 1+ | 75 / 85 / 95% |
| 4 (Deload) | 5 / 5 / 5 | 40 / 50 / 60% |

"+" = AMRAP set. Minimum rep targets: Week 1 set = 5 reps, Week 2 set = 3 reps, Week 3 set = 1 rep. Beating minimum by a wide margin (10+ reps on Week 1) is expected and desirable.

## TM Progression

After completing one 4-week cycle:
- **Upper body lifts** (OHP, Bench): TM + 2.5 kg
- **Lower body lifts** (Squat, Deadlift): TM + 5 kg

Do not chase TM increases faster than this. Wendler emphasizes patience — a 2.5 kg upper body increase per month = 30 kg per year.

## Assistance Work

Wendler's BBB template prescribes "First Set Last" or simple pull/push/single-leg assistance:
- **Push**: 3–5 sets of 10–25 push-ups or dips
- **Pull**: 3–5 sets of 5–15 pull-ups or rows (most important assistance category)
- **Single-leg/core**: Lunges, step-ups, or ab work 3–5 sets

Do not over-complicate assistance. The main + supplemental work is the program; assistance is maintenance.

## What Makes BBB Work

The 5×10 at 50% TM creates metabolic fatigue and muscle damage via high volume at a submaximal load. The key insight is that hypertrophy does not require heavy loads — it requires sufficient mechanical tension and metabolic stress to stimulate satellite cell activity. 5×10 at 50% TM with 60–90 second rest provides exactly this, while preserving the ability to perform the heavy main work productively.

> Related programs: `phul` (similar strength + hypertrophy split), `phat` (more volume, advanced). Related guides: `bodybuilding_intermediate_guide`.

# Arnold Split

The Arnold Split reflects the training philosophy of Arnold Schwarzenegger's prime competitive years (1970–1975). The key insight is pairing antagonist muscle groups — chest with back, shoulders with arms — which allows natural superset organization: while the chest is being pressed, the back recovers; while the biceps are being curled, the triceps recover. This allows higher total volume per session without compromising quality, and the twice-per-week frequency for each muscle group aligns with modern evidence on optimal training frequency for hypertrophy.

## Who This Is For

- Intermediate trainees who have completed a full-body or upper-lower program and want to increase training frequency and volume
- Athletes interested in the Golden Era aesthetic: broad shoulders, full chest, narrow waist, and proportional development
- Those who can commit to 6 training days per week (the program does not function on 5 or fewer days)

## Weekly Schedule

| Day | Focus | Key Movement Pairing |
|-----|-------|---------------------|
| Monday | Chest + Back | Bench ↔ Row (antagonist superset) |
| Tuesday | Shoulders + Arms | Press ↔ Curl+Extension |
| Wednesday | Legs | Squat → Leg Press → Isolation |
| Thursday | Chest + Back | Same as Monday (heavier or varied) |
| Friday | Shoulders + Arms | Same as Tuesday (heavier or varied) |
| Saturday | Legs | Same as Wednesday |
| Sunday | Rest | — |

## Chest + Back Day (Monday and Thursday)

| Exercise | Sets × Reps | Pairing |
|----------|------------|---------|
| Bench Press | 4 × 6–10 | Superset A1 |
| Bent-over Row | 4 × 6–10 | Superset A2 |
| Incline Dumbbell Press | 3 × 8–12 | Superset B1 |
| Pull-ups | 3 × 8–12 | Superset B2 |
| Dumbbell Flyes | 3 × 10–15 | Superset C1 |
| Seated Cable Row | 3 × 10–15 | Superset C2 |

## Shoulders + Arms Day (Tuesday and Friday)

| Exercise | Sets × Reps | Pairing |
|----------|------------|---------|
| Overhead Press (Arnold Press) | 4 × 6–10 | — |
| Barbell Curl | 4 × 6–10 | Superset A1 |
| Triceps Pushdown | 4 × 8–12 | Superset A2 |
| Side Lateral Raise | 4 × 10–15 | — |
| EZ Bar Skull Crusher | 3 × 8–12 | Superset B1 |
| Dumbbell Concentration Curl | 3 × 10–15 | Superset B2 |
| Front Dumbbell Raise | 3 × 10–15 | — |

## Leg Day (Wednesday and Saturday)

| Exercise | Sets × Reps |
|----------|------------|
| Back Squat | 4 × 8–12 |
| Leg Press | 3 × 10–15 |
| Leg Extensions | 3 × 12–15 |
| Lying Leg Curl | 3 × 10–15 |
| Standing Calf Raise | 4 × 15–20 |

## Arnold's Training Principles

1. **Mind-muscle connection**: Visualize the target muscle contracting with each repetition; mental focus increases EMG activation of the intended muscle
2. **Full range of motion**: Partial reps shortchange the stretch-mediated hypertrophic stimulus; Arnold always insisted on full ROM
3. **Shock the muscle periodically**: Vary training order, angles, or loading scheme every 4–6 weeks to prevent accommodation
4. **Priority training**: Train weakest body parts first in the session when CNS freshness is maximal

## Progression

- **Compound lifts**: Linear load increase when all reps are achieved with good form
- **Isolation lifts**: Double-progression (advance within rep range, then add load)
- **Every 4 weeks**: Rotate one exercise per muscle group to introduce novel stimulus
- **Deload (Week 9)**: 50% volume for one week

> Related programs: `push_pull_legs` (less volume, same frequency concept), `phul` (power-hypertrophy hybrid). Related guides: `bodybuilding_intermediate_guide`.

# Beginner Full-Body Hypertrophy

A three-day-per-week full-body program designed for trainees with less than 6–12 months of consistent resistance training. Every session trains every major muscle group, which maximizes weekly frequency while keeping per-session volume manageable. Beginners adapt primarily through neural mechanisms in the first 4–8 weeks and accumulate muscle mass from 8–12 weeks onward; training each pattern three times per week accelerates motor learning for the compound movements that form the long-term foundation.

## Who This Is For

- Trainees with < 1 year of consistent barbell/dumbbell training
- Anyone returning after a layoff > 6 months
- Trainees transitioning from machine-based training to free weights
- Those who cannot commit to 4–6 day splits

**Not appropriate for**: intermediate trainees who have stalled on beginner linear progression (use PHUL, PPL, or RP Hypertrophy instead).

## Schedule

Sessions rotate A/B every training day:

| Week | Mon | Wed | Fri |
|------|-----|-----|-----|
| 1 | A | B | A |
| 2 | B | A | B |
| 3 | A | B | A |
| … | … | … | … |

Rest at least one day between sessions. Weekend rest is flexible — Mon/Wed/Fri is conventional but Tue/Thu/Sat works equally well.

## Session A

| Exercise | Sets × Reps | Notes |
|----------|-------------|-------|
| Back Squat | 3 × 6–10 | Primary lower-body compound |
| Bench Press | 3 × 8–12 | Primary horizontal push |
| Bent-over Row | 3 × 8–12 | Primary horizontal pull |
| Romanian Deadlift | 3 × 10–12 | Posterior chain |
| Barbell Curl | 3 × 10–15 | Elbow flexors |
| Triceps Pushdown | 3 × 12–15 | Triceps |
| Side Lateral Raise | 3 × 15–20 | Lateral deltoid |

**Session A duration**: 55–70 minutes including warm-up sets.

## Session B

| Exercise | Sets × Reps | Notes |
|----------|-------------|-------|
| Leg Press | 3 × 10–15 | Secondary quad compound |
| Incline Dumbbell Press | 3 × 10–12 | Upper chest angle variation |
| Pull-ups | 3 × 5–8 (or to failure) | Primary vertical pull |
| Overhead Press | 3 × 8–12 | Vertical push compound |
| Hammer Curls | 3 × 12–15 | Brachialis and brachioradialis |
| Lying Triceps Press | 3 × 12–15 | Triceps long head |
| Face Pull | 3 × 15–20 | Rear delt / shoulder health |

**Session B duration**: 55–70 minutes including warm-up sets.

## Warm-Up Protocol

Before each session:
1. 5 min light cardio (row, bike, or brisk walk)
2. For the first compound movement only, ramp up: 50% × 8, 70% × 5, 85% × 2, then working sets
3. Subsequent exercises need only 1 light warm-up set

## Progression

**Linear double-progression**:
1. Start every exercise at the bottom of its rep range with a weight that leaves 3–4 reps in reserve (RIR) on the last set
2. Each session: attempt to beat the previous session's rep count at the same weight
3. When the top of the rep range is achieved for all sets: add weight next session, reset to bottom of range
4. If minimum reps cannot be completed: hold weight, try again next session
5. After two failed sessions at the same weight: reduce load by 10%, rebuild

**Example — Bench Press at 60 kg**:
- Session 1: 3 × 8 reps → goal is to hit 8, 8, 8
- Session 3: 60 kg × 8, 8, 10 → still below top (12); continue at 60 kg
- Session 5: 60 kg × 10, 11, 12 → all sets hit top of range → next session: 62.5 kg × 8, 8, 8

Beginners typically progress every session or every other session on compound lifts. This rate slows after 8–12 weeks.

## Pull-up Scaling

If fewer than 5 unassisted pull-ups can be performed:
- Use a resistance band looped around the bar (reduces effective bodyweight)
- Or substitute lat pulldown at equivalent load
- Progress by gradually reducing band resistance or increasing lat pulldown weight

Do not use kipping or momentum — full range, controlled tempo.

## When to Move On

Progress to an intermediate program (PHUL, PPL, or RP Hypertrophy) when:
- Linear progression on compounds stalls for 2–3 consecutive weeks despite adequate sleep and nutrition
- Approximately 12 weeks of consistent training at this program
- Squat ≥ 1× bodyweight, Bench ≥ 0.7× bodyweight, Row ≥ 0.75× bodyweight (rough benchmarks)

> For nutrition support see `energy_balance`, `protein_requirements`, and `nutrient_timing`. For recovery context see `sleep` and `deload_protocol`. Intermediate options: `phul`, `push_pull_legs`, `rp_hypertrophy`.

- ref: back_squat
  role: primary
  technical_notes: 'Lower A: 3×6-10. Primary quad-dominant compound. High bar position,
    descend to at least parallel. Brace hard before each rep. Focus on consistent
    depth before adding load.'
- ref: romanian_deadlift
  role: primary
  technical_notes: 'Lower A: 3×10-12. Primary hip hinge. Maintain flat back throughout.
    Push hips back until hamstring stretch limits ROM, then drive hips forward to
    lockout. Do not squat the weight.'
- ref: leg_press
  role: secondary
  technical_notes: 'Lower A: 3×10-15. Secondary quad work. Feet mid-plate, shoulder
    width. Do not lock knees at the top; do not let lower back round off the pad at
    the bottom.'
- ref: seated_calf_raise
  role: accessory
  technical_notes: 'Lower A: 3×12-15. Soleus emphasis. Full stretch at the bottom,
    2-second pause at peak contraction. Calves require high effort per set — take
    the last set close to failure.'
- ref: incline_dumbbell_press
  role: primary
  technical_notes: 'Upper B: 3×8-12. Upper chest press variation at 30-45° incline.
    Varies the pressing angle from flat bench on Upper A. Full ROM: dumbbells to chest
    level, press to full lockout.'
- ref: seated_cable_rows
  role: primary
  technical_notes: 'Upper B: 3×10-12. Horizontal pull variation. Neutral or close
    grip. Pull to lower sternum, squeeze shoulder blades, control the return. Keep
    torso upright — do not rock.'
- ref: face_pull
  role: secondary
  technical_notes: 'Upper B: 3×15-20. Rear deltoid and external rotation. Pull rope
    to face level with elbows high. Non-negotiable for shoulder health alongside pressing
    volume.'
- ref: hammer_curls
  role: accessory
  technical_notes: 'Upper B: 2×10-15. Brachialis and brachioradialis. Neutral grip,
    alternating or simultaneous. Full ROM, controlled tempo.'
- ref: cable_rope_overhead_triceps_extension
  role: accessory
  technical_notes: 'Upper B: 2×12-15. Tricep long head emphasis. Face away from cable,
    extend overhead. Stretch fully at the bottom of each rep.'
- ref: conventional_deadlift
  role: primary
  technical_notes: 'Lower B: 3×5-8. Primary posterior chain compound. Heavier and
    lower rep than RDL. Set up with hips higher than knees, shoulders over the bar,
    lats tight. Each rep starts from a dead stop on the floor.'
- ref: front_squat
  role: secondary
  technical_notes: 'Lower B: 3×8-12. Quad-dominant squat variation. Clean grip or
    cross-arm grip. Requires more thoracic mobility than back squat. Prioritize upright
    torso and full depth. Start light and build.'
- ref: lying_leg_curls
  role: secondary
  technical_notes: 'Lower B: 3×10-15. Hamstring isolation. Full ROM: extend fully
    at the top, curl fully. Do not lift hips off the pad. Control the eccentric.'
- ref: standing_calf_raises
  role: accessory
  technical_notes: 'Lower B: 3×10-15. Gastrocnemius emphasis (straight knee). Full
    stretch at the bottom, full contraction at the top. Pair with seated calf raise
    on Lower A to cover both heads.'
- ref: hanging_leg_raise
  role: accessory
  technical_notes: 'Lower B: 3×10-15. Core work. Hang from a pull-up bar, raise legs
    to at least 90°. If full leg raises are too difficult, start with knee raises
    and progress over weeks.'
sources:
- title: 'Resistance Training Recommendations to Maximize Muscle Hypertrophy in an
    Athletic Population: Position Stand of the IUSCA'
  credibility: expert_consensus
- title: Science and Development of Muscle Hypertrophy (2nd Edition)
  credibility: expert_consensus
- title: 'Effects of Resistance Training Frequency on Measures of Muscle Hypertrophy:
    A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis'
  credibility: meta_analysis

# Beginner Upper/Lower Split

A four-day-per-week program that splits training into upper body and lower body sessions, each performed twice per week. This structure provides more per-session volume and exercise variety than the three-day full-body approach while maintaining the twice-weekly frequency per muscle group that Schoenfeld et al. (2016, meta-analysis) found optimal for hypertrophy.

## Who This Is For

- Trainees with < 1 year of consistent training who can commit to 4 days per week
- Beginners who find full-body sessions too long or fatiguing (3-day full-body programs compress 14+ exercises into 3 sessions)
- Anyone who prefers a more focused session: fewer movement patterns per day, more attention to each
- Trainees who want a structured stepping stone toward intermediate splits (PPL, PHUL)

**Not appropriate for**: trainees who can only train 3 days per week (use Beginner Full-Body Hypertrophy instead) or intermediate trainees who have exhausted beginner linear progression.

## Schedule

| Day | Session | Focus |
|-----|---------|-------|
| Monday | Upper A | Press-dominant (bench, OHP, row, pull-up) |
| Tuesday | Lower A | Squat-dominant (back squat, RDL, leg press) |
| Wednesday | Rest | |
| Thursday | Upper B | Press-variation (incline DB, cable row, face pull) |
| Friday | Lower B | Hinge-dominant (deadlift, front squat, leg curl) |
| Sat–Sun | Rest | |

The day labels are flexible — any arrangement with at least one rest day between same-type sessions works (e.g., Mon/Tue/Thu/Fri, Tue/Wed/Fri/Sat).

## Upper A

| Exercise | Sets × Reps | Notes |
|----------|-------------|-------|
| Bench Press | 3 × 6–10 | Primary horizontal press |
| Bent-Over Barbell Row | 3 × 8–12 | Primary horizontal pull |
| Overhead Press | 3 × 8–12 | Primary vertical press |
| Pull-ups | 3 × 5–8 | Primary vertical pull (band-assist if needed) |
| Dumbbell Bicep Curl | 2 × 10–15 | Bicep isolation |
| Triceps Pushdown | 2 × 12–15 | Tricep isolation |
| Side Lateral Raise | 2 × 15–20 | Lateral deltoid |

**Session duration**: 50–65 minutes including warm-up.

## Lower A

| Exercise | Sets × Reps | Notes |
|----------|-------------|-------|
| Back Squat | 3 × 6–10 | Primary quad compound |
| Romanian Deadlift | 3 × 10–12 | Hip hinge / hamstrings |
| Leg Press | 3 × 10–15 | Secondary quad volume |
| Seated Calf Raise | 3 × 12–15 | Soleus emphasis |

**Session duration**: 40–50 minutes including warm-up.

## Upper B

| Exercise | Sets × Reps | Notes |
|----------|-------------|-------|
| Incline Dumbbell Press | 3 × 8–12 | Upper chest variation |
| Seated Cable Rows | 3 × 10–12 | Horizontal pull variation |
| Face Pull | 3 × 15–20 | Rear delt / shoulder health |
| Hammer Curls | 2 × 10–15 | Brachialis / forearm |
| Cable Overhead Tricep Extension | 2 × 12–15 | Tricep long head |

**Session duration**: 40–55 minutes including warm-up.

## Lower B

| Exercise | Sets × Reps | Notes |
|----------|-------------|-------|
| Conventional Deadlift | 3 × 5–8 | Primary posterior chain compound |
| Front Squat | 3 × 8–12 | Quad-dominant variation |
| Lying Leg Curl | 3 × 10–15 | Hamstring isolation |
| Standing Calf Raise | 3 × 10–15 | Gastrocnemius emphasis |
| Hanging Leg Raise | 3 × 10–15 | Core |

**Session duration**: 45–55 minutes including warm-up.

## Warm-Up Protocol

Before each session:
1. 5 min light cardio (row, bike, or brisk walk)
2. For the first compound movement only, ramp up: 50% × 8, 70% × 5, 85% × 2, then working sets
3. Subsequent compounds need only 1 light warm-up set; isolation exercises need no warm-up

## Progression

**Linear double-progression** (identical to Beginner Full-Body Hypertrophy):
1. Start every exercise at the bottom of its rep range with a weight that leaves 3–4 RIR on the last set
2. Each session: attempt to beat the previous session's rep count at the same weight
3. When all sets hit the top of the rep range: add weight next session, reset to bottom of range
4. If minimum reps cannot be completed: hold weight, try again next session
5. After two failed sessions at the same weight: reduce load by 10%, rebuild

**Weight increments**:
- Upper body compounds (bench, OHP, row): +2.5 kg
- Lower body compounds (squat, deadlift): +5 kg
- Isolation exercises: +1.25–2.5 kg (or add 1–2 reps per set before increasing weight)

## Why Upper/Lower Instead of Full-Body

Both structures work for beginners. The choice depends on schedule and preference:

| Factor | Full-Body 3x | Upper/Lower 4x |
|--------|-------------|----------------|
| Days per week | 3 | 4 |
| Exercises per session | 7 | 4–7 |
| Session duration | 55–70 min | 40–65 min |
| Volume per muscle/week | Equal | Equal (distributed across 2 sessions) |
| Fatigue management | Higher per-session (everything in one day) | Lower per-session (split across days) |
| Exercise variety | Lower (same exercises every session) | Higher (A/B variation within each split) |

## When to Move On

Progress to an intermediate program (PHUL, PPL, RP Hypertrophy) when:
- Linear progression on compounds stalls for 2–3 consecutive weeks despite adequate sleep and nutrition
- Approximately 12 weeks of consistent training at this program
- Squat ≥ 1× bodyweight, Bench ≥ 0.7× bodyweight, Deadlift ≥ 1.25× bodyweight (rough benchmarks)

> For nutrition support see `energy_balance`, `protein_requirements`, and `body_recomposition`. For recovery context see `sleep` and `deload_protocol`. Intermediate options: `phul`, `push_pull_legs`, `rp_hypertrophy`.

# Bodybuilding Isolation Block

A 6-week, 4-day-per-week specialisation program built entirely from isolation exercises. Each session focuses on a single muscle region; no compound movements are prescribed. The program is designed for intermediate and advanced bodybuilders who have an established strength base and want to bring up lagging isolation muscles — particularly chest detail, shoulder roundness, arm development, and leg isolation work.

## Who This Program Is For

- Intermediate/advanced bodybuilders running a compound strength base (e.g., 5/3/1, Texas Method) as a concurrent isolation layer
- Athletes completing a strength block who want a hypertrophy-focused deload/transition phase
- Bodybuilders with specific lagging body parts that compound movements under-develop

**Not for beginners.** Isolation work produces limited overall hypertrophy compared to compound movements. Beginners should run `beginner_fullbody_hypertrophy` or `push_pull_legs` first.

## Weekly Structure

| Day | Session | Total working sets |
|-----|---------|--------------------|
| Monday | Day A — Chest Isolation | 14–18 sets |
| Tuesday | Day B — Shoulders Isolation | 12–16 sets |
| Thursday | Day C — Arms (Biceps + Triceps) | 18–24 sets |
| Saturday | Day D — Legs Isolation | 14–18 sets |

Rest at least one day between sessions. The Tuesday/Thursday gap is intentional — shoulders (Day B) need 48 h before the triceps overhead work on Day C.

## How to Select Exercises Per Day

Each day lists a **core group** (primary + secondary) and an **optional group** (accessory). Run the core group every session. Rotate accessory exercises in and out every 2–3 weeks based on lagging areas.

### Day A — Chest Isolation

**Core (choose 4):**

| Exercise | Sets × Reps | Key cue |
|----------|-------------|---------|
| Dumbbell Fly | 4 × 10–15 | Slight fixed elbow bend; control the eccentric |
| Incline Dumbbell Fly | 3 × 12–15 | 30–45° bench; upper pec bias |
| Flat Bench Cable Fly | 3 × 12–15 | Constant tension; low pulleys |
| Butterfly (Pec Deck) | 3 × 12–15 | Peak contraction + 1-second squeeze |

**Optional (rotate in 1–2):**

| Exercise | Sets × Reps | When to use |
|----------|-------------|-------------|
| Decline Dumbbell Fly | 2–3 × 12–15 | Lower pec lagging |
| Incline Cable Fly | 2–3 × 12–15 | Prefer constant tension over stretch |
| One-Arm Flat Bench Dumbbell Fly | 2 × 12–15/side | Left/right imbalance present |

### Day B — Shoulders Isolation

**Core (all 4):**

| Exercise | Sets × Reps | Key cue |
|----------|-------------|---------|
| Seated Side Lateral Raise | 4 × 12–20 | Raise to shoulder height only; elbow leads |
| Cable Seated Lateral Raise | 3 × 15–20 | Constant tension in bottom range |
| Seated Bent-Over Rear Delt Raise | 4 × 15–20 | Torso parallel to floor; elbows lead |
| Front Cable Raise | 3 × 12–15 | Only if anterior delt is genuinely lagging |

**Optional:**

| Exercise | Sets × Reps | When to use |
|----------|-------------|-------------|
| Bent-Over DB Rear Delt Raise (head on bench) | 2–3 × 15–20 | Strictest rear delt technique; swap when lower back fatigued |

### Day C — Arms: Biceps + Triceps

Run biceps first, then triceps. Choose 3–4 exercises from each group.

**Biceps (core — choose 3):**

| Exercise | Sets × Reps | Key cue |
|----------|-------------|---------|
| Dumbbell Bicep Curl | 4 × 10–15 | Supinate at midpoint; elbow fixed |
| Incline Dumbbell Curl | 3 × 10–15 | Long-head stretch; arm behind torso at bottom |
| Concentration Curl | 3 × 12–15 | Peak contraction; full supination |
| Cable Preacher Curl | 3 × 10–15 | Constant tension in lengthened position |

**Biceps (optional — rotate 1):**

| Exercise | Sets × Reps | When to use |
|----------|-------------|-------------|
| EZ Bar Curl | 3 × 10–12 | Heavier load priority; wrist comfort |
| Drag Curl | 2 × 12–15 | Brachialis and long-head emphasis |
| Chin-Up | 3 × 8–12 | Vertical pull stimulus; add if strength base needed |

**Triceps (core — choose 3–4):**

| Exercise | Sets × Reps | Key cue |
|----------|-------------|---------|
| Cable Rope Overhead Extension | 4 × 12–15 | Long head fully lengthened; elbows close |
| Decline EZ Bar Triceps Extension | 3 × 10–12 | Long head stretch; controlled descent |
| Cable Lying Triceps Extension | 3 × 12–15 | Constant tension at lockout |
| Tricep Dumbbell Kickback | 2 × 15–20 | Full lockout; 1-second peak hold |

**Triceps (optional — rotate 1):**

| Exercise | Sets × Reps | When to use |
|----------|-------------|-------------|
| Kneeling Cable Triceps Extension | 2–3 × 15–20 | Strictest form; no lower body involvement |
| Cable One-Arm Triceps Extension | 2 × 15–20/arm | Bilateral imbalance present |

### Day D — Legs Isolation

**Core (choose 4–5):**

| Exercise | Sets × Reps | Key cue |
|----------|-------------|---------|
| Lying Leg Curl | 4 × 10–15 | Plantarflex ankle during curl; slow eccentric |
| Single-Leg Leg Extension | 3 × 12–15/leg | Full extension; do not snap to lockout |
| Glute-Ham Raise | 3 × 6–10 | Hips extended; band assistance if needed |
| Standing Calf Raise | 4 × 15–20 | Full heel drop; 3–4 s eccentric |
| Donkey Calf Raise | 3 × 15–20 | Deep stretch; hip-hinge position |

**Optional:**

| Exercise | Sets × Reps | When to use |
|----------|-------------|-------------|
| Standing Leg Curl | 3 × 12–15/leg | Substitute or add for extra hamstring unilateral volume |

## Progression Over 6 Weeks

| Weeks | Strategy |
|-------|----------|
| 1–2 | Establish baselines. Use loads where the top of the rep range is just achievable. |
| 3–4 | Apply double-progression: advance load when top of range achieved. Introduce one optional exercise per day. |
| 5–6 | Max accumulation. Add one set to core exercises if recovery allows. Rotate optional exercises to address weakest area. |

## Integration With a Compound Program

This block pairs with any 2–3 day/week heavy compound program (e.g., 5/3/1 Mon/Wed/Fri):

```
Mon: Compound strength (5/3/1 squat + deadlift)
Tue: Day A — Chest Isolation
Wed: Compound strength (5/3/1 bench + press)
Thu: Day B — Shoulders Isolation
Fri: Compound strength (5/3/1 squat + deadlift)
Sat: Day C — Arms
Sun: Day D — Legs Isolation
```

Rest/frequency can be adjusted — the isolation days are low-systemic-fatigue and recover quickly.

> For compound-based hypertrophy, see `push_pull_legs` or `phul`. For power-hypertrophy blend, see `phat`.

# Bro Split (5-Day Body-Part Split)

The bro split — one muscle group per day across five sessions — is the most derided and most practiced training structure in bodybuilding history simultaneously. Its central trade-off is undeniable: each muscle group is hit only once per week, which modern meta-analyses show is suboptimal for frequency, but the per-session volume concentration generates intense metabolic stress, muscle damage, and the classic "pump" that practitioners report produces faster hypertrophic gains than they experience with higher-frequency programs. The research verdict is that twice-per-week frequency is superior on average, but the bro split remains valuable for intermediate athletes with high per-session recovery capacity, limited weekly schedule flexibility, or the need for a psychological break from technically demanding programs.

## Who This Is For

- Intermediate trainees (1–2 years of consistent training) who have completed full-body or upper-lower programs and want to increase per-session volume dramatically
- Those who can only train at consistent times on 5 weekdays and cannot easily rearrange their schedule to optimize 48-hour muscle recovery windows
- Bodybuilders entering a hypertrophy specialization phase who want to experience maximum per-session stimulus on each muscle group

## Weekly Schedule

| Day | Focus | Primary Compound |
|-----|-------|-----------------|
| Monday | Chest | Bench Press |
| Tuesday | Back | Deadlift + Row |
| Wednesday | Shoulders | Overhead Press |
| Thursday | Arms | Barbell Curl + Pushdown |
| Friday | Legs | Back Squat |
| Saturday/Sunday | Rest | — |

## Chest Day (Monday)

| Exercise | Sets × Reps | Notes |
|----------|------------|-------|
| Bench Press | 4 × 6–10 | Compound opener |
| Incline Dumbbell Press | 4 × 8–12 | Upper chest |
| Dumbbell Flyes | 3 × 12–15 | Stretch emphasis |
| Cable Crossover | 3 × 15–20 | Peak contraction finisher |

## Back Day (Tuesday)

| Exercise | Sets × Reps | Notes |
|----------|------------|-------|
| Conventional Deadlift | 4 × 4–8 | Primary compound |
| Bent-over Barbell Row | 4 × 6–10 | Horizontal pull |
| Wide-grip Pull-ups | 3 × 8–12 | Lat width |
| Seated Cable Row | 3 × 10–15 | Mid-back thickness |
| Lat Pulldown | 3 × 12–15 | Lat isolation finisher |

## Shoulder Day (Wednesday)

| Exercise | Sets × Reps | Notes |
|----------|------------|-------|
| Overhead Press | 4 × 6–10 | Primary compound |
| Side Lateral Raise | 4 × 12–15 | Lateral delt width |
| Rear Delt Fly | 4 × 12–15 | Posterior delt health |
| Front Dumbbell Raise | 3 × 12–15 | Anterior delt |

## Arm Day (Thursday)

| Exercise | Sets × Reps | Notes |
|----------|------------|-------|
| Barbell Curl | 4 × 6–10 | Bicep compound |
| Tricep Pushdown | 4 × 10–15 | Tricep compound |
| Incline Dumbbell Curl | 3 × 10–15 | Bicep long head |
| EZ Bar Skull Crusher | 3 × 8–12 | Tricep long head |
| Concentration Curl | 3 × 12–15 | Bicep peak |
| Overhead Tricep Extension | 3 × 12–15 | Tricep long head stretch |

## Leg Day (Friday)

| Exercise | Sets × Reps | Notes |
|----------|------------|-------|
| Back Squat | 4 × 6–10 | Compound opener |
| Leg Press | 4 × 10–15 | Quad volume |
| Romanian Deadlift | 3 × 8–12 | Hamstring/glute |
| Leg Extensions | 3 × 12–15 | Quad isolation |
| Seated Leg Curl | 3 × 10–15 | Hamstring isolation |
| Standing Calf Raise | 4 × 15–20 | Calf mass |

## Progression

- **Compound lifts**: Linear load increase when all prescribed reps completed with good form at the top of the rep range
- **Isolation lifts**: Double-progression — advance reps within the given range, then add load (2.5 kg) at the next session
- **Stalling**: If load progress stalls for 2 consecutive sessions on a compound lift, reduce load 10% and build back up
- **Deload (Weeks 9 and optionally 5)**: Reduce to 60% of total working sets for one week at maintained loads

## Frequency Trade-off

The evidence-based critique of the bro split is valid: once-per-week frequency per muscle group produces less hypertrophy per unit of training time than twice-per-week in most meta-analyses. However, the bro split has practical advantages:

1. Each session is laser-focused — the trainee knows exactly what they are training and why
2. High per-session volume provides maximal metabolic stress on the target muscle
3. Weekly structure is simple to follow without needing to track muscle recovery windows
4. Many experienced intermediate athletes report superior results on the bro split compared to lower-volume, higher-frequency approaches — likely due to individual recovery differences

The bro split works best for athletes who prioritize pump-based training, have high caloric intake and recovery support, or have previously plateaued on higher-frequency programs.

> Related programs: `arnold_split` (higher frequency via antagonist pairings), `push_pull_legs` (3-day grouping for similar volume at higher frequency), `upper_lower_4day` (best evidence-based balance of frequency and volume). Related guides: `bodybuilding_intermediate_guide`.

# DC Training (Doggcrapp)

Created by Dante Trudel and popularized through internet bodybuilding forums in the early 2000s, DC Training is built on three unconventional principles: (1) rest-pause sets as the primary intensity technique instead of multiple straight sets, (2) extreme stretching of the worked muscle for 60–90 seconds immediately after each exercise, and (3) exercise rotation to continuously present new mechanical stimuli as accommodation develops. The system produces unusually fast strength gains alongside hypertrophy in advanced athletes who have stalled on traditional volume-based programs.

## Who This Is For

- Advanced bodybuilders who have run intermediate programs for 2+ years and have stalled on conventional volume-based programming
- Athletes with a strength base: Bench ≥ 1.25× BW, Squat ≥ 1.75× BW, Row ≥ 1× BW
- Not for: beginners or early intermediates — the rest-pause intensity and high frequency (every 4–5 days per muscle) creates profound fatigue that beginners cannot recover from

## The Rest-Pause Set

DC uses a single rest-pause set per exercise rather than multiple straight sets:

1. Load the bar for approximately 8–12 rep max
2. Perform reps to volitional concentric failure
3. Rack the bar / re-rack the weight
4. Take 15 deep diaphragmatic breaths (approximately 20–25 seconds)
5. Perform reps to failure again
6. 15 breaths again
7. Final set to failure
8. Record total reps across all three mini-sets

At the next session: beat the total rep count. When total reps plateau for 3 consecutive sessions at the same weight, rotate to the next exercise in that muscle group's rotation.

## Extreme Stretching Protocol

Immediately after each rest-pause set, perform a 60–90 second extreme stretch for the worked muscle:

| Muscle | Extreme Stretch Position |
|--------|-------------------------|
| Chest | Dumbbell flye bottom position with 5–10 kg dumbbell, elbow at 90°, held for 60–90 sec |
| Lats/Back | Straight-arm lat hang from pull-up bar, bodyweight, 60–90 sec |
| Biceps | Arms behind torso, hands on bench, body forward, 60–90 sec |
| Triceps | Overhead extension bottom position with light dumbbell, held 60–90 sec |
| Quads | Sissy squat bottom position or kneeling quad stretch, 60–90 sec |
| Hamstrings | Standing RDL bottom position with light load, 60–90 sec |
| Calves | Heel off step in full dorsiflexion, 30–60 sec per calf |

Extreme stretching in DC is not passive yoga-style stretching — it is weighted or loaded stretching that applies mechanical tension to the fully elongated muscle, activating titin mechanosensing and providing stretch-mediated hypertrophic stimulus.

## Weekly Structure (3 Days Per Week)

| Week | Monday | Wednesday | Friday |
|------|--------|-----------|--------|
| Odd | Workout A | Workout B | Workout A |
| Even | Workout B | Workout A | Workout B |

Each muscle group is trained every 4–5 days — more frequent than typical bodybuilding splits but less frequent than daily training, allowing DC's high-intensity rest-pause format to fully recover.

## Workout A

| Body Part | Exercise | Protocol |
|-----------|---------|---------|
| Chest | Bench Press | 1 × rest-pause + 60-sec extreme stretch |
| Back (width) | Bent-over Barbell Row | 1 × rest-pause + 60-sec lat hang |
| Shoulders | Seated Dumbbell Press | 1 × rest-pause |
| Triceps | Close-grip Bench | 1 × rest-pause + overhead stretch |
| Biceps | Barbell Curl | 1 × rest-pause + stretch |
| Quads | Leg Press | 1 × rest-pause + quad stretch |
| Hamstrings | Romanian Deadlift | 2 × 8–12 straight sets |
| Calves | Standing Calf Raise | 1 × rest-pause + 30-sec extreme stretch |

## Workout B

| Body Part | Exercise | Protocol |
|-----------|---------|---------|
| Chest | Incline Dumbbell Press | 1 × rest-pause + stretch |
| Back (thickness) | Rack Pull | 1 × 6–10 straight + 60-sec lat hang |
| Shoulders | Seated Barbell Press | 1 × rest-pause |
| Triceps | Pushdown | 1 × rest-pause + stretch |
| Biceps | Incline Dumbbell Curl | 1 × rest-pause + stretch |
| Quads | Hack Squat | 1 × rest-pause + quad stretch |
| Hamstrings | Lying Leg Curl | 2 × 10–15 straight sets |
| Calves | Seated Calf Raise | 1 × rest-pause + 30-sec extreme stretch |

## Mini-Blast / Cruise Periodization

- **Blast (5–6 weeks)**: Full rest-pause intensity every session
- **Cruise (1–2 weeks)**: Light training at 15–20 rep straight sets, no failure; allows joints, tendons, and CNS to recover
- Repeat blast/cruise cycles

> Related programs: `rp_hypertrophy` (volume-based alternative), `phat` (power-hypertrophy hybrid). Related guides: `bodybuilding_advanced_guide`.

# German Volume Training (GVT)

German Volume Training is attributed to Charles Poliquin, who popularized the 10×10 protocol as a hypertrophy shock method that German national weightlifters used during the 1970s off-season to gain muscle mass rapidly. The protocol's power derives from an unusual combination: extremely high per-session volume (100 reps per muscle group) at a moderate intensity (60% 1RM) — a zone that generates maximal metabolic stress and muscle damage while remaining technically manageable across all 10 sets.

## Who This Is For

- Intermediate bodybuilders who have hit a hypertrophy plateau on their current program and need a volume-shock stimulus
- Athletes transitioning between strength blocks who want a dedicated hypertrophy phase (6 weeks)
- Note: GVT is not a long-term program — 6 weeks is the intended maximum; strength temporarily decreases under this volume load and must be rebuilt afterward

## The 10×10 Protocol Rules

1. **Load**: 60% of 1RM — this is approximately a 20RM load, which means the first 5 sets feel easy
2. **Rep tempo**: 4-0-2-0 (4 seconds down, 0 pause, 2 seconds up, 0 pause)
3. **Rest between antagonist pairs**: 90 seconds (from end of A1 to start of A2, and from end of A2 to start of A1)
4. **Load does not change** across all 10 sets — even if you fail, reduce rest to 60 seconds but keep the load
5. **When 10×10 is completed clean**: increase load by 2.5% at the next session
6. **Accessory work**: maximum 2–3 sets of 2 exercises; GVT volume is sufficient stimulus

## Weekly Schedule

| Day | Pairing | Target Muscles |
|-----|---------|---------------|
| Monday | Chest/Back 10×10 | Pecs, Lats/Rhomboids |
| Tuesday | Rest | — |
| Wednesday | Legs 10×10 | Quads, Hamstrings |
| Thursday | Rest | — |
| Friday | Shoulders/Upper Back 10×10 | Deltoids, Lats (vertical) |
| Saturday | Arms + Posterior Chain 10×10 | Biceps, Triceps, Hamstrings |
| Sunday | Rest | — |

## Day 1: Chest and Back

| Exercise | Sets × Reps | Rest | Notes |
|----------|------------|------|-------|
| A1: Bench Press | 10 × 10 | 90 sec | 60% 1RM, paired with A2 |
| A2: Bent-over Row | 10 × 10 | 90 sec | 60% 1RM, immediately after A1 |
| B1: Incline DB Press | 3 × 10–12 | 60 sec | Accessory only |
| B2: Pull-ups | 3 × 10–12 | 60 sec | Accessory only |

## Day 2: Legs

| Exercise | Sets × Reps | Rest | Notes |
|----------|------------|------|-------|
| A1: Back Squat | 10 × 10 | 90 sec | 60% 1RM |
| A2: Lying Leg Curl | 10 × 10 | 90 sec | 60% 1RM |
| B1: Leg Extensions | 3 × 10–15 | 60 sec | Accessory |
| B2: Seated Calf Raise | 3 × 15–20 | 60 sec | Accessory |

## Day 3: Shoulders and Upper Back

| Exercise | Sets × Reps | Rest | Notes |
|----------|------------|------|-------|
| A1: Overhead Press | 10 × 10 | 90 sec | 60% 1RM |
| A2: Lat Pulldown | 10 × 10 | 90 sec | 60% 1RM |

## Day 4: Arms and Posterior Chain

| Exercise | Sets × Reps | Rest | Notes |
|----------|------------|------|-------|
| A1: Barbell Curl | 10 × 10 | 90 sec | 60% 1RM |
| A2: Tricep Pushdown | 10 × 10 | 90 sec | 60% 1RM |
| B1: Romanian Deadlift | 3 × 10–12 | 60 sec | Accessory; heavier than 60% |

## What to Expect

- **Week 1**: Sets 1–5 feel too easy; sets 8–10 become genuinely hard
- **Week 2**: Full-body DOMS especially after leg day; performance temporarily decreases
- **Week 3**: Adaptation begins; able to complete all 10×10 with control
- **Weeks 4–6**: Progressive small load increases become possible
- **After GVT**: Take 2–4 weeks of lower-volume, higher-intensity work to rebuild strength before running again

> Related programs: `rp_hypertrophy` (more sustainable volume approach), `phul` (strength-volume balance). Related guides: `bodybuilding_intermediate_guide`.

  (lower body) when all working sets are completed in the prescribed range. Hypertrophy
  days: double-progression. Advance within the rep range before adding load. Deload
  every 6 weeks: reduce working sets by 40%, maintain intensity.

  '

# PHAT (Power Hypertrophy Adaptive Training)

Designed by Layne Norton, PHAT allocates the training week into two dedicated power days and three hypertrophy days. Power days develop maximal strength on the competition lifts at sub-5 rep intensities; hypertrophy days follow the same movement patterns at higher volume and moderate loads. The logic is that high-strength individuals have more contractile tissue to stimulate hypertrophy from, and maintaining strength alongside hypertrophy work prevents the detraining of neural efficiency that occurs on pure bodybuilding splits.

## Who This Is For

- Trainees who have run intermediate programs (PPL, PHUL) and have stalled on linear progression
- Those who want to develop both competition-level strength and aesthetics simultaneously
- Minimum baseline: Squat ≥ 1.5× BW, Bench ≥ 1× BW, Row ≥ 1× BW

## Weekly Schedule

| Day | Focus |
|-----|-------|
| Monday | Upper Body Power |
| Tuesday | Lower Body Power |
| Wednesday | Rest |
| Thursday | Back & Shoulders Hypertrophy |
| Friday | Lower Body Hypertrophy |
| Saturday | Chest & Arms Hypertrophy |
| Sunday | Rest |

## Upper Body Power (Monday)

| Exercise | Sets × Reps |
|----------|-------------|
| Bench Press | 3–4 × 3–5 |
| Weighted Pull-ups → Bent-over Row | 2–3 × 3–5 |
| Overhead Press | 2–3 × 5–8 |
| Incline Dumbbell Press | 3 × 6–10 |
| Barbell Curl | 3 × 6–10 |

## Lower Body Power (Tuesday)

| Exercise | Sets × Reps |
|----------|-------------|
| Back Squat | 3–4 × 3–5 |
| Romanian Deadlift | 2–3 × 5–8 |
| Leg Press | 2 × 10–15 |
| Seated Leg Curl | 3 × 6–10 |
| Seated Calf Raise | 2 × 12–15 |

## Back & Shoulders Hypertrophy (Thursday)

| Exercise | Sets × Reps |
|----------|-------------|
| Pull-ups | 3 × 8–12 |
| Bent-over Row | 4 × 8–12 |
| Seated Cable Row | 3 × 8–12 |
| Side Lateral Raise | 4 × 12–15 |
| Face Pull | 3 × 15–20 |
| Front Dumbbell Raise | 2 × 12–15 |

## Lower Body Hypertrophy (Friday)

| Exercise | Sets × Reps |
|----------|-------------|
| Back Squat | 4 × 8–12 |
| Romanian Deadlift | 3 × 8–12 |
| Leg Press | 3 × 12–15 |
| Leg Extensions | 3 × 12–15 |
| Seated Leg Curl | 3 × 10–15 |
| Seated Calf Raise | 3 × 12–15 |

## Chest & Arms Hypertrophy (Saturday)

| Exercise | Sets × Reps |
|----------|-------------|
| Bench Press | 4 × 8–12 |
| Incline Dumbbell Press | 3 × 8–12 |
| EZ Bar Skull Crusher | 3 × 8–12 |
| Triceps Pushdown | 3 × 10–15 |
| Barbell Curl | 3 × 8–12 |
| Hammer Curls | 3 × 10–15 |
| Preacher Curl | 2 × 10–15 |

## Progression

- **Power days**: when all sets hit the top of the rep range, add 2.5 kg (upper) / 5 kg (lower) at the next power session
- **Hypertrophy days**: double-progression within rep range, then add load
- **Deload (Week 7)**: drop to 60% of normal working sets; maintain intensity; resume week 8 at full volume

> Prerequisite intermediate programs: `phul`, `push_pull_legs`. Nutrition at this training age: `energy_balance`, `protein_requirements`.

# PHUL — Power Hypertrophy Upper Lower

Developed by Dr. Layne Norton. Combines low-rep strength work (power days) with high-rep hypertrophy work across a four-day upper/lower split, targeting both neural adaptations and metabolic hypertrophy simultaneously.

## Weekly Schedule

| Day | Session | Rep Range | Focus |
|-----|---------|-----------|-------|
| Monday | Upper Power | 3–5 | Strength / neural |
| Tuesday | Lower Power | 3–5 | Strength / neural |
| Wednesday | Rest | — | — |
| Thursday | Upper Hypertrophy | 8–12 | Volume / metabolic |
| Friday | Lower Hypertrophy | 8–15 | Volume / metabolic |
| Sat/Sun | Rest | — | — |

## Day 1 — Upper Power

| Exercise | Sets × Reps |
|----------|-------------|
| Bench Press | 3-4 × 3-5 |
| Incline Dumbbell Press | 3-4 × 6-10 |
| Bent Over Barbell Row | 3-4 × 3-5 |
| Weighted Pull-ups | 2-3 × 6-8 |
| Overhead Press | 2-3 × 5-8 |
| Barbell Curl | 2-3 × 6-8 |

## Day 2 — Lower Power

| Exercise | Sets × Reps |
|----------|-------------|
| Back Squat | 3-4 × 3-5 |
| Conventional Deadlift | 3-4 × 3-5 |
| Leg Press | 3-4 × 10-15 |
| Lying Leg Curl | 2-3 × 6-8 |
| Calf Raise | 4 × 6-10 |

## Day 3 — Upper Hypertrophy

| Exercise | Sets × Reps |
|----------|-------------|
| Incline Barbell Press | 3-4 × 8-12 |
| Flat Dumbbell Press | 3-4 × 8-12 |
| Seated Cable Row | 3-4 × 8-12 |
| One-Arm Dumbbell Row | 3-4 × 8-12 |
| Dumbbell Lateral Raise | 3-4 × 8-12 |
| Triceps Pushdown | 2-3 × 8-12 |
| Dumbbell Curl | 2-3 × 8-12 |

## Day 4 — Lower Hypertrophy

| Exercise | Sets × Reps |
|----------|-------------|
| Front Squat or Hack Squat | 3-4 × 8-12 |
| Romanian Deadlift | 3-4 × 8-12 |
| Leg Press | 3-4 × 12-15 |
| Leg Extension | 3-4 × 12-15 |
| Lying Leg Curl | 3-4 × 12-15 |
| Seated Calf Raise | 4 × 8-12 |

## Progression

- **Power days:** +2.5–5 kg when all reps completed with clean form
- **Hypertrophy days:** add reps; increase weight when top of range reached across all sets
- **Deload:** every 4–6 weeks, cut volume and load ~40% for one week

> For higher-volume autoregulated hypertrophy, see `rp_hypertrophy`. For pure strength, see `5_3_1`.

# Push Pull Legs (PPL)

A six-day per week split grouping muscles by movement pattern: pushing muscles (chest, shoulders, triceps) train together, pulling muscles (back, biceps, rear delts) train together, and legs (quads, hamstrings, glutes, calves) train together. Each group is hit twice per week.

## Design Philosophy — Why Push/Pull/Legs

PPL is the most popular intermediate bodybuilding split for two reasons:

**Muscle group frequency:** Each muscle group is trained twice per week. Schoenfeld et al. (2016, meta-analysis) found that training a muscle 2×/week produces significantly more hypertrophy than 1×/week at the same total volume. PPL achieves this naturally — Push A on Monday trains chest, then Push B on Thursday trains it again.

**Minimal overlap fatigue:** By grouping muscles that work together (chest + shoulders + triceps all push; back + biceps both pull), each session avoids pre-fatiguing muscles needed for the next day. After a Pull day, your pushing muscles are fully recovered for Push day the next morning. Compare this to a full-body program where every session fatigues everything, or an upper/lower split where bench press fatigues triceps before overhead press.

**Why 6 days, not 3?** A 3-day PPL (Push-Pull-Legs once each) only hits each muscle once per week — below the optimal frequency. The 6-day version doubles the frequency, which is where the hypertrophy advantage emerges. If you can only train 3–4 days, an upper/lower split is a better choice for the same reason.

**Why it works for intermediates but not beginners:** PPL distributes 18+ exercises across 6 sessions. A beginner doesn't need this volume — they grow on 10–12 exercises at 3 sessions/week. PPL becomes appropriate when a beginner's full-body program stalls and more volume per muscle group is needed to continue progressing.

## Weekly Schedule

| Day | Session |
|-----|---------|
| Monday | Push A (heavy — 4-6 reps on compounds) |
| Tuesday | Pull A (heavy) |
| Wednesday | Legs A (heavy) |
| Thursday | Push B (moderate — 8-12 reps) |
| Friday | Pull B (moderate) |
| Saturday | Legs B (moderate) |
| Sunday | Rest |

## Push Day A (Heavy)

| Exercise | Sets × Reps |
|----------|-------------|
| Bench Press | 4 × 4-6 |
| Overhead Press | 3 × 8-12 |
| Incline Dumbbell Press | 3 × 8-12 |
| Cable Lateral Raise | 3 × 10-15 |
| Triceps Pushdown | 3 × 10-15 |
| Overhead Triceps Extension | 2 × 10-15 |

## Pull Day A (Heavy)

| Exercise | Sets × Reps |
|----------|-------------|
| Bent Over Barbell Row | 4 × 4-6 |
| Weighted Pull-ups | 3 × 8-12 |
| Face Pull | 3 × 10-15 |
| Barbell Curl | 3 × 8-12 |
| Hammer Curl | 2 × 10-15 |

## Legs Day A (Heavy)

| Exercise | Sets × Reps |
|----------|-------------|
| Back Squat | 4 × 4-6 |
| Romanian Deadlift | 3 × 8-12 |
| Leg Press | 3 × 10-15 |
| Leg Curl | 3 × 10-15 |
| Standing Calf Raise | 4 × 8-12 |

## Push/Pull/Legs B Days

B days mirror the A-day movement patterns at 8-12 or 10-15 reps, using variations:
- **Push B:** Incline barbell, seated OHP, dumbbell fly, cable lateral raise, skull crusher
- **Pull B:** Chest-supported row, lat pulldown, cable row, preacher curl, face pull
- **Legs B:** Front squat or hack squat, single-leg RDL, leg extension, leg curl, seated calf raise

## Progression

- **Double-progression:** reach top of rep range for all sets, add 2.5 kg (upper) / 5 kg (lower)
- Start next session at bottom of rep range with new weight
- If unable to complete minimum reps: reduce load 5-10%, rebuild

## 3-Day Variant

Run Push/Pull/Legs once per week (Mon/Wed/Fri) for lower-frequency hypertrophy training.

> Each muscle group trained 2x/week. For autoregulated volume, see `rp_hypertrophy`. For power-hypertrophy blend, see `phul`.

# RP Hypertrophy Program

The Renaissance Periodization Hypertrophy Program (Israetel, Hoffman & Smith 2021) uses RIR-based autoregulation and MEV→MRV volume progression to systematically drive hypertrophy while managing accumulated fatigue within and across mesocycles.

## 4-Day Upper/Lower Split (Week 1 Baseline)

| Day | Focus | Key Exercises | Sets × Reps | RIR |
|-----|-------|---------------|-------------|-----|
| 1 — Upper A | Chest / Triceps / Back / Biceps | Incline DB Fly, Cable Triceps Ext, Seated Cable Row, Cable Biceps Curl | 2×10-15 each | 3 |
| 2 — Lower A | Quads / Hamstrings / Calves / Glutes / Delts | Back Squat, Lying Leg Curl, Calf Press, Hip Abductor, Cable Lateral Raise | 2×6-10 / 2×10-15 | 3 |
| 3 — Upper B | Back / Biceps / Chest | Pull-Up, EZ-Bar Preacher Curl, Incline Bench Press | 2×6-10 each | 3 |
| 4 — Lower B | Hamstrings / Glutes / Calves | Romanian Deadlift, Barbell Hip Thrust, Standing Calf Raise | 2×8-12 each | 3 |

## RIR Progression

| Week | Target RIR | Notes |
|------|-----------|-------|
| 1 | 3 | Establish baseline, validate weights |
| 2 | 2 | Moderate overload; add sets if recovery permits |
| 3 | 1 | Near-limit effort; assess joint and systemic fatigue |
| 4 | 0–1 | Peak overload; selected sets to concentric failure |
| 5 (Deload) | 4–5 | First half: 70% peak load; second half: 50% peak load; volume 50% of W1 |

## Volume Auto-Regulation Scale

| Rating | Recovery State | Action |
|--------|---------------|--------|
| +2 | No soreness, no pump | Add 1-2 sets next session |
| +1 | Recovered ahead of schedule | Add 1 set |
| 0 | Resolved just in time | Maintain current volume |
| -1 | Slightly sore or stalled | Minor volume reduction |
| -2 | Severely under-recovered | Significant volume cut |

## Fractional Set Counting (Compound Lifts)

| Exercise | Primary (1.0 set) | Secondary (0.5 set) | Tertiary (0.5 set) |
|----------|-------------------|---------------------|---------------------|
| Barbell Bench Press | Pectoralis Major | Anterior Deltoid | Triceps Brachii |
| Bent Over Barbell Row | Latissimus Dorsi | Posterior Deltoid | Biceps Brachii |
| Back Squat | Quadriceps | Gluteus Maximus | — |

## Multi-Mesocycle Block Structure

1. **Basic Hypertrophy** (2 × 5-week cycles = 10 weeks): 60–80% 1RM, 8–12 reps
2. **Metabolite Focus** (1 × 5-week cycle): 30–50% 1RM, 15–30 reps; myo-reps, drop sets, occlusion
3. **Resensitization** (3 weeks): MV only (~6 sets/week/muscle at 3 RIR); resets cellular sensitivity

> For muscle-group volume landmarks (MEV, MAV, MRV), see `core/volume_landmarks.md`.

- title: Muscle hypertrophy response to training volumes in untrained males and females
  credibility: rct
---

# Upper Lower 4-Day Split

The Upper Lower Split is one of the most evidence-aligned training structures for intermediate hypertrophy development. By dividing the body into upper (chest, back, shoulders, arms) and lower (quads, hamstrings, glutes, calves) and training each twice per week, it achieves the 2× weekly frequency that meta-analyses consistently show as superior to 1× for hypertrophy outcomes — while keeping weekly session count manageable at 4 days.

## Who This Is For

- Trainees who have completed a beginner full-body program (Starting Strength, GZCLP, 3-day full-body) and are ready for more volume and specialization
- Those who can train 4 days per week with 48 hours between upper sessions and 48 hours between lower sessions
- Not for: complete beginners (start with full-body); advanced trainees who need higher specificity per muscle group (use PPL, PHAT, or Arnold Split)

## Weekly Schedule Options

**Option A (Most Common):**
| Day | Session |
|-----|---------|
| Monday | Upper A (Heavy) |
| Tuesday | Lower A (Heavy) |
| Wednesday | Rest |
| Thursday | Upper B (Volume) |
| Friday | Lower B (Volume) |
| Saturday/Sunday | Rest |

**Option B (Weekend Lifter):**
| Day | Session |
|-----|---------|
| Monday | Upper A (Heavy) |
| Wednesday | Lower A (Heavy) |
| Friday | Upper B (Volume) |
| Saturday | Lower B (Volume) |

## Upper A — Heavy Day (Monday)

| Exercise | Sets × Reps |
|----------|------------|
| Bench Press | 4 × 4–6 |
| Bent-over Row (overhand) | 4 × 4–6 |
| Overhead Press (standing) | 3 × 6–8 |
| Weighted Pull-ups | 3 × 6–8 |
| Barbell Curl | 3 × 8–12 |
| Face Pull | 3 × 15–20 |

## Lower A — Heavy Day (Tuesday)

| Exercise | Sets × Reps |
|----------|------------|
| Back Squat | 4 × 4–6 |
| Romanian Deadlift | 3 × 6–8 |
| Leg Press | 2 × 8–12 |
| Seated Leg Curl | 3 × 8–10 |
| Standing Calf Raise | 3 × 12–15 |

## Upper B — Volume Day (Thursday)

| Exercise | Sets × Reps |
|----------|------------|
| Incline Dumbbell Press | 3 × 8–12 |
| Cable Row (close grip) | 3 × 10–15 |
| Dumbbell Overhead Press | 3 × 10–15 |
| Lat Pulldown | 3 × 10–15 |
| Tricep Pushdown | 3 × 10–15 |
| Face Pull | 3 × 15–20 |

## Lower B — Volume Day (Friday)

| Exercise | Sets × Reps |
|----------|------------|
| Back Squat | 3 × 8–12 |
| Romanian Deadlift | 3 × 10–12 |
| Leg Press | 3 × 12–15 |
| Leg Extensions | 3 × 12–15 |
| Seated Leg Curl | 3 × 10–15 |
| Standing Calf Raise | 3 × 12–15 |

## Progression

- **Heavy days (A)**: Add 2.5 kg (upper) or 5 kg (lower) when all reps completed at top of range
- **Volume days (B)**: Double-progression; advance within rep range, then add 2.5 kg
- **Deload (Week 7)**: Drop to 3 sets per exercise, maintain loads; resume Week 8 at full volume

> Beginner prerequisite programs: `beginner_fullbody_hypertrophy`. Advanced progressions: `push_pull_legs`, `phat`. Related guides: `bodybuilding_intermediate_guide`.

# BWF Recommended Routine

Community-vetted, milestone-based full-body calisthenics program for beginners and early-intermediate trainees. Requires only a pull-up bar and parallel bars (or rings). Session duration typically 45–60 minutes.

## Design Philosophy — Why Progressions Instead of Weight

Barbell programs add weight to increase difficulty. Bodyweight programs can't do that — your body weight is fixed. The BWF Recommended Routine solves this through **progression chains**: sequences of increasingly difficult variations of the same movement pattern.

**Example:** For horizontal push, the chain is: wall push-up → incline push-up → regular push-up → diamond push-up → pseudo planche push-up → planche push-up. Each step increases the mechanical disadvantage (lever length, balance demand, or range of motion), which increases the load on the target muscles without adding external weight.

**Why paired sets?** The routine pairs opposing movement patterns (push + pull, or squat + hinge) and alternates between them with rest in between. This doubles the number of exercises you can fit into the same time without fatiguing the same muscles — while one muscle group works, the other recovers. Research supports that paired-set training produces equivalent strength and hypertrophy gains to traditional sets in less total time.

**Why 3×5–8 and not higher reps?** The routine prescribes 3 sets of 5–8 reps. When you can do 3×8, you advance to the next progression where you'll be back at 3×5. This keeps every set in the strength-hypertrophy zone (see `rep_continuum`) and ensures that each progression step is genuinely challenging. If reps go above 12–15, the exercise becomes too easy and the stimulus shifts toward endurance — which is not the goal.

**Why it works for beginners:** The progression chains provide a built-in level-adjustment system. A complete beginner starts at the easiest variation (wall push-up) and a stronger beginner starts further along the chain (regular push-up). Both follow the same program structure — the starting point is different, but the progression logic is identical.

## Session Structure

3 sessions per week on non-consecutive days (Mon/Wed/Fri or Tue/Thu/Sat).

1. **Warm-up** (5-10 min): shoulder band drill, squat sky reaches, wrist prep, deadbugs. Once tier thresholds are reached, arch hangs and support hold shift into the warm-up block.
2. **Paired strength work** (superset format, 90s rest between each exercise):
   - Pair 1: Vertical Pull ↔ Knee-Dominant
   - Pair 2: Horizontal Pull ↔ Hip-Dominant
   - Pair 3: Vertical Push ↔ Horizontal Push
3. **Core triplet** (at session end — after compound work to protect spinal stability)

## Upper Body Progressions

| Vector | Tier | Exercise | Advancement |
|--------|------|----------|-------------|
| Vertical Pull | 1 | Scapular Pulls | 3×8 |
| | 2 | Arch Hangs | 3×8 |
| | 3 | Negative Pull-ups | 3×8 (5-10s descent) |
| | 4 | Pull-ups | 3×8 |
| | 5 | Weighted Pull-ups | Load-based linear |
| Horizontal Pull | 1 | Vertical Rows | 3×8 |
| | 2 | Incline Rows | 3×8 |
| | 3 | Horizontal Rows | 3×8 |
| | 4 | Wide Rows (1.5× grip) | 3×8 |
| | 5 | Archer Rows | 3×8 per side |
| Vertical Push | 1 | Support Hold | 3×60s |
| | 2 | Negative Dips | 3×8 |
| | 3 | Parallel Bar Dips | 3×8 |
| | 4 | Ring Dips | 3×8 |
| Horizontal Push | 1 | Wall Push-ups | 3×8 |
| | 2 | Incline Push-ups | 3×8 |
| | 3 | Full Push-ups | 3×8 |
| | 4 | Diamond Push-ups | 3×8 |
| | 5 | Pseudo Planche Push-ups | 3×8 with lean |

## Lower Body Progressions

| Vector | Tier | Exercise | Advancement |
|--------|------|----------|-------------|
| Knee-Dominant | 1 | Assisted Squat | 3×8 |
| | 2 | Air Squat | 3×8 |
| | 3 | Split Squat | 3×8 per leg |
| | 4 | Bulgarian Split Squat | 3×8 per leg |
| | 5 | Shrimp Squat | 3×8 per leg |
| | 6 | Pistol Squat | 3×8 per leg |
| Hip-Dominant | 1 | Romanian Deadlift | 3×8 |
| | 2 | Single-Leg Deadlift | 3×8 per leg |
| | 3 | Banded Nordic Curl Negative | 3×8 |
| | 4 | Nordic Curl | 3×8 |

## Core Triplet (always performed last)

| Category | Progression | Target |
|----------|-------------|--------|
| Anti-extension | Plank → Ring Ab Rollouts → Ab Wheel | 3×30s / 3×8-12 |
| Anti-rotation | Banded Pallof Press → Ring Pallof Press | 3×8-12 |
| Extension | Reverse Hyperextension → Glute-Ham Raise | 3×8-12 |

## Hybrid Barbell Option

Replace lower-body calisthenics for trainees with squat rack access:

| Day | Exercise | Sets × Reps |
|-----|----------|-------------|
| Mon & Fri | Barbell Back Squat | 3×5 |
| | Romanian Deadlift | 3×8 |
| Wed | Barbell Back Squat | 3×5 |
| | Conventional Deadlift | 3×5 |

## Key Rules

- Advance tier only when 3×8 achieved with clean form (or 30s/60s for holds)
- If current tier is too hard for 3×5: drop one tier, build volume, retry
- Stop each set 1-2 reps before technical form breakdown
- Tempo 1-0-X-0 on every dynamic rep — no momentum cheating

# BWF Advanced Skill Day Template

The r/bodyweightfitness advanced skill day template emerged from the community's collective experience that the linear progression model of the BWF Recommended Routine — advancing when 3 × 5 reps are achievable — does not translate well to high-tension isometric skills like planche and front lever. These skills have different neuromuscular demands: the joint angle specificity is extreme, recovery requirements are different from dynamic exercises, and the progression steps are separated by months rather than weeks.

The community developed three distinct skill day templates to address these differences:

**Template 1: Statics Pairing** — Planche and front lever holds are paired within the same set, alternating with 2.5–5 minute rest between each skill. This allows near-complete ATP recovery for each hold while protecting the elbow joint from unidirectional repetitive loading (planche stresses the anterior elbow in extension; front lever stresses the posterior elbow in flexion — alternating them provides natural antagonist recovery).

**Template 2: Upper/Lower Skill Split** — Skill work (planche, front lever, handstand) is placed at the beginning of sessions when the motor cortex is freshest. Dynamic strength work (pull-ups, dips) follows after skill practice. This mirrors the OG principle that skill practice requires a fresh CNS.

**Template 3: Daily Undulating Periodization (DUP)** — Hold duration and difficulty vary across three weekly sessions (heavy/moderate/light). This template is documented in this file and is the most structured of the three.

## Who This Is For

- Intermediate to advanced athletes who have plateaued on linear skill progression
- Athletes currently capable of: 3-second tuck planche, 5-second tuck front lever, 5-second freestanding handstand
- This is a **modular template**, not a complete program — it integrates into an existing 4–5 day training split as the skill component

## The DUP Rotation (3 Days/Week)

| Session Type | Hold Duration | Progression Step | Rest Between Sets |
|-------------|--------------|-----------------|-------------------|
| Heavy | 1–4 s | Hardest achievable | 4–5 min |
| Moderate | 6–10 s | Current or 1 step easier | 3–4 min |
| Light | 10–16 s | 2 steps easier | 2–3 min |

Example for planche (athlete at Tuck Planche):
- Heavy: Advanced Tuck Planche holds, 1–4 s × 6 sets
- Moderate: Tuck Planche holds, 6–10 s × 5 sets
- Light: Planche Lean + Tuck raises, 10–16 s × 4 sets

## The Statics Pairing Format (within each session)

When both planche and front lever are trained:

```
Set 1 — Planche hold (prescribed duration)
Rest 2.5 min
Set 1 — Front Lever hold (prescribed duration)
Rest 2.5 min
Set 2 — Planche hold
...
```

The alternating pattern provides 5 minutes total between same-skill sets while keeping the session time efficient. The planche-front lever pairing is the classic antagonist pair in straight-arm calisthenics — they can always share a session.

## Elbow Health Constraint

The primary injury risk in this template is elbow flexor overuse from combined planche and front lever volume. Management rules:
- Planche is sequenced first (greater CNS demand; anterior elbow stress)
- Front lever is always sequenced second
- Heavy day: skill holds only; no supplemental pulling after front lever
- If medial or lateral elbow pain develops: drop two progression steps on both skills immediately

## Key Rules

- **Heavy day holds are maximal quality, not maximal duration.** The 1–4 second hold on the heavy day is not a time limit — it is the duration that can be held with full position quality. If the position degrades in the first second, the set is over.
- **Light day is not a deload.** Light day provides accumulated volume at easier progressions — it is adaptation stimulus, not recovery. Total weekly isometric volume is higher on a three-day DUP schedule than on a simple two-day heavy schedule.
- **Test maximum hold duration monthly, not weekly.** The DUP structure produces strength gains that appear in monthly assessments — daily assessment creates false plateaus by measuring short-term fatigue, not long-term adaptation.

> Preceding programs: `calisthenics_intermediate_skills`, `overcoming_gravity_template`. Advanced strength support: `calisthenics_advanced_skills`.

# Calisthenics Advanced Strength Skills

Advanced calisthenics targets static strength skills (planche, front lever, handstand) and elite dynamic skills (ring muscle-up, one-arm pull-up progressions). These movements require force production in positions where the body's lever arm against gravity is maximized — making them among the most demanding strength feats achievable with bodyweight. The training adaptation timeline is longer than any other program in this knowledge base: tendons, ligaments, and specific motor pathways for these positions require 12–24 months of consistent work, and the program reflects this by being 24 weeks.

## Who This Is For

- Athletes who have completed `calisthenics_intermediate_skills` or equivalent
- Minimum baselines: 5 strict ring muscle-ups, 15-second L-sit, freestanding handstand hold 5 s, weighted pull-up +30 kg
- Training age in calisthenics: 2+ years

## Core Skill Targets

| Skill | Entry (start) | Goal (week 24) |
|-------|--------------|----------------|
| Planche | Tuck planche hold 10 s | Straddle planche 5 s |
| Front Lever | Tuck front lever 10 s | Straddle front lever 10 s |
| Handstand | Freestanding hold 5 s | HSPU 3 reps (freestanding) |
| Ring Muscle-up | 5 strict | 5 weighted (+5 kg) |
| One-arm Pull-up | Archer pull-up | Negatives + assisted one-arm |

## 5-Day Schedule

| Day | Primary Skills | Strength Support |
|-----|---------------|-----------------|
| Mon | Planche progressions + Front Lever | Weighted Pull-ups + Ring Dips |
| Tue | Handstand + One-arm progression | Push-up variations + Inverted Row |
| Wed | Planche + Muscle-up | Weighted Pull-ups + Ring Dips |
| Thu | Handstand + L-sit / V-sit | Parallel Bar Dips + Pallof Press |
| Fri | Front Lever + Planche | Weighted Pull-ups + Ring Dips |
| Sat–Sun | Rest or active mobility |  |

## Skill Practice Format

Each skill session follows this structure:
1. **Activation**: scapular pull-ups + hollow body (5 min)
2. **Max quality reps**: 5–8 working sets of the skill progression at max quality, stopping before form degrades
3. **Volume sets**: 2–3 back-off sets at one step easier progression, higher reps

**Planche progression ladder** (each step requires 3 × 8 s with control):
Tuck (bent knee) → Advanced Tuck (knee to elbow) → Straddle → Full

**Front Lever progression ladder** (each step requires 3 × 8 s):
Tuck → Advanced Tuck → One-leg → Straddle → Full

**Handstand push-up ladder** (each step: 3 × 5 reps):
Elevated pike → Wall HSPU (head touches) → Wall HSPU (floor) → Freestanding negatives → Freestanding HSPU

## Connective Tissue Management

Planche and front lever training concentrate extraordinary force at the shoulder, elbow, and wrist in extended lever positions. These structures adapt more slowly than the muscles generating the force. Mandatory protocols:
- **Gelatin + Vitamin C** before every session (see `connective_tissue`)
- **Scapular pull-ups** every session without exception
- **Never train a skill to failure**: stop 2 reps before form breaks down
- If elbow or wrist pain develops: reduce progression difficulty by two steps immediately; do not train through tendon pain

Expect 3–6 months of building tolerance before working sets feel "normal" on planche and front lever progressions.

> Entry point: `calisthenics_intermediate_skills`. Injury prevention: `connective_tissue`, `load_management`. Recovery: `active_recovery`, `sleep`.

# Calisthenics Intermediate Skill Development

After establishing the base pulling, pushing, and core capacity from beginner programs (BWF Recommended Routine), intermediate calisthenics shifts toward **skill acquisition**. The target movements at this stage — ring muscle-up, handstand push-up, pistol squat, and L-sit — require both the strength to produce the movement and the technical coordination to execute it correctly. Training must address both dimensions.

## Who This Is For

- Trainees who have completed BWF Recommended Routine or equivalent
- Minimum baselines: 10 strict pull-ups, 15 push-ups with full range, 30-second dead hang, 5 parallel bar dips
- Goals: ring muscle-up, handstand push-up, L-sit to tuck planche progressions

## 4-Day Schedule

| Day | Focus |
|-----|-------|
| Monday | Pull strength + Ring push |
| Tuesday | Horizontal push/pull + Core |
| Wednesday | Skill practice only (low volume, fresh CNS) |
| Thursday | Pull strength + Ring push |
| Friday | Horizontal push/pull + Core |
| Sat–Sun | Rest or Zone 2 cardio |

## Skill Work (Wednesday — Dedicated Skill Day)

Wednesday is for **technique practice**, not strength accumulation. Low fatigue, fresh nervous system:

| Skill | Target | Progression ladder |
|-------|--------|--------------------|
| Muscle-up | 3–5 reps | Ring support hold → bar muscle-up negatives → band-assisted muscle-up → strict muscle-up |
| Handstand push-up | 5 reps | Pike push-up → elevated pike → wall HSPU → freestanding HSPU |
| L-sit | 10–15 s | Tuck L-sit → single-leg L-sit → full L-sit on parallettes |
| Pistol squat | 5 per side | Box pistol → assisted pistol → full pistol |

Spend 10–15 minutes on each skill, then stop. Skill practice with fatigue reinforces poor patterns.

## Monday / Thursday Session

| Exercise | Sets × Reps |
|----------|-------------|
| Scapular Pull-ups (activation) | 3 × 12 |
| Weighted Pull-ups | 4 × 4–6 |
| Ring Dips | 4 × 5–8 |
| Hollow Body Hold | 3 × 20 s |

## Tuesday / Friday Session

| Exercise | Sets × Reps |
|----------|-------------|
| Archer Push-ups | 3 × 5–8/side |
| Inverted Row (feet elevated) | 3 × 8–12 |
| Parallel Bar Dips | 3 × 8–12 |
| Pallof Press | 3 × 10/side |

## Progression Rules

1. Move to next skill variation only after 3 consecutive sessions completing 3 × 5 with controlled form
2. Never train a skill to failure — stop 2–3 reps before form breaks
3. Wrist, elbow, and shoulder soreness is common; see `connective_tissue` for management
4. Gelatin + Vitamin C protocol (pre-training) is particularly valuable for ring work and wrist-loading skills

> Entry point: `bwf_recommended_routine`. For advanced skill targets: `calisthenics_advanced_skills`. Injury context: `connective_tissue`, `load_management`.

# Convict Conditioning Big Six

*Convict Conditioning* (2011) by Paul "Coach" Wade presents a minimalist bodyweight training system built around six fundamental human movement patterns, each with a ten-step progression from complete beginner to elite. The stated philosophy is that modern gym culture has abandoned the systematic progression logic of older physical culture, replacing it with machine isolation and external load that bypasses the body's own adaptive progression.

The system requires no equipment beyond a pull-up bar and a floor. The six movements — Push, Pull, Squat, Leg Raise, Bridge, and Handstand Push-up — are presented as the complete set of movements needed to develop "supreme strength." No isolation work, no machines, no additional accessories.

The most distinctive aspect of the system is its **deliberate pace**. Unlike programs that rush toward advanced movements, Convict Conditioning insists on mastering each step completely before advancement. The stated reason is connective tissue: tendons, ligaments, and joint capsules adapt far more slowly than muscle tissue, and skipping steps means arriving at the advanced progressions with musculature strong enough to produce the movement but connective tissue too underdeveloped to sustain the load safely.

## Who This Is For

- Absolute beginners with no prior training
- Athletes training without equipment (home, travel)
- Intermediate athletes seeking to develop advanced unilateral movements (one-arm pull-up, one-arm push-up, pistol squat)
- Anyone interested in the classic physical culture approach to bodyweight development

## Big Six Overview

| Series | Entry (Step 1) | Master Step (Step 10) |
|--------|---------------|----------------------|
| Push (horizontal) | Wall Push-up | One-Arm Push-up |
| Pull (vertical) | Jackknife Pull-up | One-Arm Pull-up |
| Squat (knee-dominant) | Shoulderstand Squat | Pistol Squat |
| Leg Raise (core) | Lying Knee Tuck | Hanging Windshield Wiper |
| Bridge (posterior chain) | Short Bridge | Stand-to-Stand Bridge |
| Handstand Push-up (overhead) | Wall Headstand Hold | One-Arm Handstand Push-up |

## Weekly Training Schedule (3-Day Rotation)

Convict Conditioning prescribes a **big-little** alternating schedule:

| Day | Series Trained |
|-----|---------------|
| Monday | Push + Pull |
| Wednesday | Legs (Squat + Leg Raise) |
| Friday | Bridge + Handstand Push-up |

All six series are trained three times per week on this schedule (each twice per week). The system also provides a more frequent 6-day schedule for athletes who want to train each series more often.

## Advancement Criteria (Consolidation Standards)

Advance to the next step only when the current consolidation standard is met for **three consecutive training sessions**:

| Step | Movement | Consolidation Standard |
|------|----------|----------------------|
| 1 | Wall Push-up | 3 × 50 |
| 5 | Full Push-up | 3 × 30 |
| 10 | One-Arm Push-up | 1 × 100 per arm |
| 5 | Full Pull-up | 3 × 15 |
| 10 | One-Arm Pull-up | 1 × 6 per arm |
| 5 | Full Squat | 3 × 50 |
| 10 | Pistol Squat | 2 × 50 per leg |
| 5 | Hanging Knee Raise | 3 × 30 |
| 8 | Hanging Straight Leg Raise | 3 × 20 |

## Key Rules

- **Connective tissue pace, not muscle pace.** The advancement criteria are high-rep standards deliberately chosen to ensure connective tissue has fully adapted before the next progression is introduced. Skipping a step because "it feels too easy" is the most common cause of injury at higher steps.
- **Never train on consecutive days.** The system's rest structure is not negotiable — 48 hours between training sessions is the minimum recovery window.
- **Each step is an independent milestone.** The six series progress independently — being at Step 8 in Pull-ups while at Step 3 in Handstand Push-ups is normal and expected. Do not artificially synchronize steps.

> After completing Step 5–6 in most series, consider supplementing with `bwf_recommended_routine` or `overcoming_gravity_template` for structured pulling/pushing balance and skill-day practice.

# Get Strong: The Ultimate 16-Week Transformation Program

*Get Strong* (2017) by Al and Danny Kavadlo is the Kavadlo brothers' first book to contain a complete, structured daily training program with specific sets, reps, progressions, warm-up guides, and rest days. Their earlier books (*Raising the Bar*, *Pushing the Limits!*) are exercise encyclopedias with step-by-step progression guides but without a complete program structure.

The defining feature of *Get Strong* is its **mandatory phase-end testing protocol**: at the end of each four-week phase, the athlete must meet a specific performance standard before advancing. If the standard is not met, the athlete repeats weeks 3–4 of the current phase and tests again. This enforces strict progression and prevents the premature advancement that is the most common failure mode in self-directed calisthenics programming.

## Who This Is For

- Beginners to intermediate athletes with some movement experience
- Athletes who want a structured, time-bound (16-week) calisthenics program with clear milestones
- Minimum baseline: 5 push-ups, 1 pull-up (band-assisted is acceptable for Phase 1)

## Four-Phase Structure

| Phase | Weeks | Theme | Primary Focus |
|-------|-------|-------|---------------|
| Phase 1 | 1–4 | Foundation | Movement quality; establish base reps; learn movement standards |
| Phase 2 | 5–8 | Brick and Mortar | Increase volume; introduce harder variations |
| Phase 3 | 9–12 | Concrete and Iron | Unilateral progressions; weighted options introduced |
| Phase 4 | 13–16 | Forged from Steel | Maximum volume and difficulty; peak capacity |

## 4-Day Weekly Schedule

*Get Strong* uses a 4-day schedule with 3 rest days. Recommended layout:

| Day | Focus |
|-----|-------|
| Day 1 | Upper push + Upper pull |
| Day 2 | Lower body + Core |
| Day 3 | Rest |
| Day 4 | Full body compound + Upper push |
| Day 5 | Rest |
| Day 6 | Full body + Core |
| Day 7 | Rest |

## End-of-Phase Testing (Mandatory Before Advancement)

| Phase Test | Minimum Standard |
|-----------|-----------------|
| Phase 1 → Phase 2 | 10 push-ups, 5 pull-ups, 10 air squats, 10 hanging knee raises |
| Phase 2 → Phase 3 | 20 push-ups, 8 pull-ups, 8 Bulgarian split squats per leg, 10 hanging bent-leg raises |
| Phase 3 → Phase 4 | 10 archer push-ups per side, 10 pull-ups, 5 box pistol squats per leg, 8 hanging straight-leg raises |

*If any element of the phase test is not passed, return to weeks 3–4 of the current phase and retest.*

## Key Rules

- **Phase tests are non-negotiable.** Advancing without passing the test means entering the next phase without the structural foundation that phase was designed to build. The Kavadlos designed the failure protocol (repeat weeks 3–4) because most athletes will pass on the second attempt without significant setback.
- **Add sets, not reps, when stuck.** If prescribed reps cannot be reached, add sets at a lower rep count rather than lowering reps per set. The cumulative volume per session is the adaptation driver.
- **Push-pull balance is built into the schedule.** Do not reorder sessions to front-load either push or pull days.

> Preceding program: `bwf_recommended_routine`. For skill-specific development after this program, see `overcoming_gravity_template` or `calisthenics_intermediate_skills`.
